<div class='question question'>

Work out $1548 \div  6$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 1548 \div  6 \\\\
&= \dfrac{1548}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{258 \times 6}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{258 \times \cancel 6}{\cancel 6} \\\\
&= 258
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$258$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 1548 \div  6 \\\\
&= \dfrac{1548}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{258 \times 6}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{258 \times \cancel 6}{\cancel 6} \\\\
&= 258
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

The next term is calculated by subtracting $6$ from the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\maroonC {(-6)} } 
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(-6)} }  
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(-6)} }   
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(-6)} }   
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(-6)} }  \\
98,  &&  92,  &&  86,  &&   80,  &&   \ldots, && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}       &= 80 - 6 \\\\
                                  &= 74 \\\\
\text{Second missing number}      &= 74 - 6 \\\\
                                  &= 68
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

Work out $\dfrac{2}{3}$  of $81$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{2}{3} \ \text {of} \ 81 \\\\
&= \dfrac {2} {3} \times 81 \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 81} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 27 \times 3} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 27 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
&= 2 \times 27 \\\\
&= 54
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$54$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{2}{3} \ \text {of} \ 81 \\\\

&= \dfrac {2 \times 81} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 27 \times 3} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 27 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
&= 2 \times 27 \\\\
&= 54
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$360 \div 10 = 3.6 \times \boxed{\phantom{5555}}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $=a$

$
\begin{aligned}
360 \div 10                  &= 3.6 \times a \\\\
\dfrac {360} {10}            &= 3.6 \times a \\\\
\dfrac {360} {10 \times 3.6} &= a \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac {360} {10 \times 3.6} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac {360} {36} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac {36 \times 10} {36} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {36} \times 10} {\cancel {36}} \\\\
a                            &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Missing number $=10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing number $=a$

a                            &= \dfrac {360} {10 \times 3.6} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac {360} {36} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac {36 \times 10} {36} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {36} \times 10} {\cancel {36}} \\\\
a                            &= 10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Missing number $=10$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$24 \div (\boxed{\phantom{5555}} -  8) = 6$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $=a$

$
\begin{aligned}
24 \div (a - 8)     &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac{24}{a - 8}  &= 6 \\\\
24                  &= (a - 8) \times 6 \\\\
a - 8               &= \dfrac{24}{6} \\\\
a - 8               &= \dfrac{4 \times 6}{6} \\\\
a - 8               &= \dfrac{4 \times \cancel 6}{\cancel 6} \\\\
a - 8               &= 4 \\\\
a                   &= 8 + 4 \\\\
a                   &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

Missing number $=12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing number $=a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

a - 8               &= \dfrac{24}{6} \\\\
a - 8               &= \dfrac{4 \times 6}{6} \\\\
a - 8               &= \dfrac{4 \times \cancel 6}{\cancel 6} \\\\
a - 8               &= 4 \\\\
a                   &= 8 + 4 \\\\
a                   &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Missing number $=12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12$
</code></pre>
</div>

                        &= 12 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}

There is one number where the output is the same as the input.

What is the number?

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a - 3) \times 2 &= a \\\\
a - 3 &= \dfrac{a}{2} \\\\
a - \dfrac{a}{2} &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac{a}{2} &= 3 \\\\
a &= 3 \times 2 \\\\
a &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A different number machine produces the input and output table below.

|**input**|**output**|
|:-------:|:--------:|
|$2$      | $6$      | 
|$4$      | $7$      | 
|$8$      | $9$      | 
|$12$     | $11$     | 


What is the rule for this number machine?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the output $= x$

Let the rule $= a \times x + b$

From Row 1, where $x = 2$,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + b &= 6 \\\\
2a + b &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

From Row 2, where $x = 4$,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 4 + b &= 7 \\\\
4a + b &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve the equations for \' a \'

$
\begin{aligned}
4a + b &= 7 \\\\
4a + (6 - 2a) &= 7 \\\\
4a - 2a &= 7 - 6 \\\\
2a &= 1 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{1}{2}
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve the equations for \' b \'

$
\begin{aligned}
2a + b &= 6 \\\\
2 \times \dfrac{1}{2} + b &= 6 \\\\
1 + b &= 6 \\\\
b &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the rule $= \dfrac{x}{2} + 5$

Lets verify the same for Row 3

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{x}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= \dfrac{8}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= 4 + 5 \\\\
&= 9 = 9
\end{aligned}
$

Lets verify the same for Row 4

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{x}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= \dfrac{12}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= 6 + 5 \\\\
&= 11 = 11
\end{aligned}
$

The rule is **Divide the number by 2 and add 5**

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Divide the number by 2 and add 5

</div>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the output $= x$
<emptyline>
Let the rule $= a \times x + b$
<emptyline>
From Row 1, where $x = 2$,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + b &= 6 \\\\
2a + b &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
From Row 2, where $x = 4$,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 4 + b &= 7 \\\\
4a + b &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets solve the equations for \' a \'
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4a + b &= 7 \\\\
4a + (6 - 2a) &= 7 \\\\
4a - 2a &= 7 - 6 \\\\
2a &= 1 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{1}{2}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets solve the equations for \' b \'
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2a + b &= 6 \\\\
2 \times \dfrac{1}{2} + b &= 6 \\\\
1 + b &= 6 \\\\
b &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Therefore, the rule $= \dfrac{x}{2} + 5$
<emptyline>
Lets verify the same for Row 3
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{x}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= \dfrac{8}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= 4 + 5 \\\\
&= 9 = 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets verify the same for Row 4
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{x}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= \dfrac{12}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= 6 + 5 \\\\
&= 11 = 11
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The rule is **Divide the number by 2 and add 5**
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2:1:16</p>

<div class='question question'>

Caley starts with a number, doubles it and then subtracts $7$.

The result is $31$.

What number did Caley start with?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times a - 7 &= 31 \\\\
2 a            &= 31 + 7 \\\\
2 a            &= 38 \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {38} {2} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {19 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {19 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a              &= 19
\end{aligned}
$

Number Caley started with $=19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $=a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times a - 7 &= 31 \\\\
2 a            &= 31 + 7 \\\\
2 a            &= 38 \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {38} {2} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {19 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a              &= \dfrac {19 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a              &= 19
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number Caley started with $=19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>F858D0CE-F0D7-4B18-A22D-210D8F94DE51</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2:1:17</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g3</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>

tage

<li>

<div class='question question'>

Becky is $10 \dfrac{1}{2} \ \text{years}$ old.

Her brother is exactly $1 \ \text{year}$ and $8 \ \text{months}$ younger than Becky.

How old is her brother?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Becky's age}      &= 10 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {years} \\\\
\text{Brother's age}    &= 10 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {years} - 1 \ \text {years} \ 8 \ \text {months} \\\\
                        &= 10 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {years} - 1 \dfrac {8} {12} \ \text {years} \\\\
                        &= 10 \dfrac {1} {2} - 1 \dfrac {2} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {21} {2} - \dfrac {5} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {21 \times 3} {2 \times 3} - \dfrac {5 \times 2} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {63} {6} - \dfrac {10} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {63 - 10} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {53} {6} \\\\
                        &= 8 \dfrac {5} {6} \ \text {years} \\\\
                        &= 8 \ \text {years} \ \dfrac {5} {6} \times 12 \ \text {months} \\\\
                        &= 8 \ \text {years } 10 \ \text {months}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \ \text {years } 10 \ \text {months}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Becky's age}      &= 10 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {years} \\\\
\text{Brother's age}    &= 10 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {years} - 1 \ \text {years} \ 8 \ \text {months} \\\\
                        &= 10 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {years} - 1 \dfrac {8} {12} \ \text {years} \\\\
                        &= 10 \dfrac {1} {2} - 1 \dfrac {2} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {21} {2} - \dfrac {5} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {21 \times 3} {2 \times 3} - \dfrac {5 \times 2} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {63} {6} - \dfrac {10} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {63 - 10} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {53} {6} \\\\
                        &= 8 \dfrac {5} {6} \ \text {years} \\\\
                        &= 8 \ \text {years} \ \dfrac {5} {6} \times 12 \ \text {months} \\\\
                        &= 8 \ \text {years } 10 \ \text {months}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8 \ \text {years } 10 \ \text {months}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2:1:18</p>

<div class='question question'>

The start and finish times of a film are shown on this notice. 

| **The Tiger Prince** |                     |
|:--------------------:|:-------------------:|
|         Start        |  $7.45 \ \text{pm}$ |
|          End         | $10.34 \ \text{pm}$ |

For how long did the film last?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Film lasted} &= 10{:}34 - 7{:}45  \\\\
                    &= 2 \ \text{hours} \ 49 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2 \ \text{hours} \ 49 \ \text{minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Film lasted} &= 10{:}34 - 7{:}45  \\\\
                    &= 2 \ \text{hours} \ 49 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2 \ \text{hours} \ 49 \ \text{minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Jasper is making fairy cakes.   

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-19-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-19-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-19-00.png")

A recipe to make $24$ fairy cakes requires the following
ingredients: 



|$230 \ \text{grams}$| flour  |
|:------------------:|:------:|
|$230 \ \text{grams}$| butter |
|$230 \ \text{grams}$| sugar  |
|$4$                 | eggs   |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much flour is needed to make $12$ fairy cakes?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Flour needed}       &= \dfrac {230} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {115 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {115 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                          &= 115 \ \text{grams}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of flour needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
24 \ \text{cakes} : 230 \ \text{grams}              &= 12 \ \text{cakes} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{24} {230}                                &= \dfrac {12} {a} \\\\
24 \times a                                     &= 12 \times 230 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 230} {24} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 2 \times 115} {2 \times 12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2 \times 115} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                               &= 115 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour needed $= 115 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{fairy cakes}                                  &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 24                                                  &:& 230 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & \dfrac {24} {2}                                     &:& \dfrac{230} {2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {2 \times 12} {2}                            &:& \dfrac{2 \times 115} {2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 12} {\cancel 2}            &:& \dfrac{\cancel 2 \times 115} {\cancel 2} \\\\
= & 12                                                  &:& 115 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour needed $= 115 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$115 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                          &= \dfrac {115 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {115 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                          &= 115 \ \text{grams}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Jasper decides to make $36$ fairy cakes.

Work out the amount of each ingredient which Jasper needs.

|$\ldots \text{grams}$| flour  |
|:------------------:|:------:|
|$\ldots \text{grams}$| butter |
|$\ldots \text{grams}$| sugar  |
|$\ldots$                 | eggs   |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{fairy cakes} &:& \text{flour}                                                                                 &:& \text{eggs}                                                                                  &:& \text{butter}                                                                                  &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 24                 &:& 230 \ \text{g}                                                                               &:& 4                                                                                            &:& 230 \ \text{g}                                                                                 &:& 230 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                  &:& \dfrac {230} {24}                                                                            &:& \dfrac {4} {24}                                                                              &:& \dfrac {230} {24}                                                                              &:& \dfrac {230} {24} \\\\
= & 1 \times 36        &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24}                                                                  &:& \dfrac {4 \times 36} {24}                                                                    &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24}                                                                    &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                         &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                         &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}     &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}     &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& 115 \times 3                                                                                 &:& 2 \times 3                                                                                   &:& 115 \times 3                                                                                   &:& 115 \times 3 \\\\
= & 36                 &:& 345 \ \text{g}                                                                               &:& 6                                                                                            &:& 345 \ \text{g}                                                                                 &:& 345 \ \text{g} \\\\
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Flour $=$ $345 \ \text{grams}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Eggs $=$ $6$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Butter $=$ $345 \ \text{grams}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Sugar $=$ $345 \ \text{grams}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{fairy cakes} &:& \text{flour}                                                                                 &:& \text{eggs}                                                                                  &:& \text{butter}                                                                                  &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 24                 &:& 230 \ \text{g}                                                                               &:& 4                                                                                            &:& 230 \ \text{g}                                                                                 &:& 230 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                  &:& \dfrac {230} {24}                                                                            &:& \dfrac {4} {24}                                                                              &:& \dfrac {230} {24}                                                                              &:& \dfrac {230} {24} \\\\
= & 1 \times 36        &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24}                                                                  &:& \dfrac {4 \times 36} {24}                                                                    &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24}                                                                    &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                         &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                         &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}     &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}     &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& 115 \times 3                                                                                 &:& 2 \times 3                                                                                   &:& 115 \times 3                                                                                   &:& 115 \times 3 \\\\
= & 36                 &:& 345 \ \text{g}                                                                               &:& 6                                                                                            &:& 345 \ \text{g}                                                                                 &:& 345 \ \text{g} \\\\
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The chocolate icing for $24$ cakes uses $60 \ \text{grams}$ of cocoa 
powder. Jasper decides to put chocolate icing on only half of his $36$ fairy cakes.

How much cocoa powder does he use?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of cocoa powder used $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cakes iced}     &= \dfrac {36} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {18 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
24 \ \text{cakes} : 60 \ \text{grams}                     &= 18 \ \text{cakes} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{24} {60}                                           &= \dfrac {18} {a} \\\\
24 \times a                                               &= 18 \times 60 \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {18 \times 60} {24} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {9 \times 2 \times 5 \times 12} {2 \times 12} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel {12}} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                                         &= 9 \times 5 \\\\
a                                                         &= 45 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Cocoa powder used $= 45 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cakes iced}     &= \dfrac {36} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {18 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cakes}          &:& \text{cocoa powder} \\\\
= & 24                    &:& 60 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                     &:& \dfrac {60} {24} \\\\
= & 1 \times 18           &:& \dfrac{60 \times 18} {24} \\\\
= & 18                    &:& \dfrac{12 \times 5 \times 9 \times 2} {2 \times 12} \\\\
= & 18                    &:& \dfrac{\cancel {12} \times 5 \times 9 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {12}} \\\\
= & 18                    &:& 5 \times 9 \\\\
= & 18                    &:& 45 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Cocoa powder used $= 45 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$45 \ \text{grams}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of cocoa powder used $= a$

\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

At the end of $2011$, there were $1234$ members of Feel Fit Gym.

During the year, $167$ members had joined the gym and $38$ had left.

How many members were there at the start of $2011?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Members} &= 1234 - 167 + 38 \\\\
&= 1105
\end{aligned}
$

$1105$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<div class='question question'>

Kate originally had $1 \ \text{litre}$ of water in a jug. 

She poured out some water and the diagram shows how much water is left in the jug.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-24-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-24-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-24-00.png")

How much water has Kate poured out from the jug?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Water taken}            &= 1 \ \text{litre} - 400 \ \text{ml} \\\\
                              &= 1 \times 1000 \ \text{ml} - 400 \ \text{ml} \\\\
                              &= 1000 - 400 \\\\
                              &= 600 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$600 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Water taken}            &= 1 \ \text{litre} - 400 \ \text{ml} \\\\
                              &= 1 \times 1000 \ \text{ml} - 400 \ \text{ml} \\\\
                              &= 1000 - 400 \\\\
                              &= 600 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>5235BF01-DA75-4C84-A7A7-3B17AAB007EB</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2:1:28</p>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Kate asked some people to tell her their favourite character in the Wizard of Oz.

Here is a pie chart showing her results:

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-28-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-28-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-28-00.png")
 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What percentage of the people she asked liked Toto best ?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{\% people that liked Toto best}       &= \dfrac {2} {8} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 100} {8} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 25 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 25 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
                                            &= 25 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{\% people that liked Toto best}       &= \dfrac {2} {8} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 100} {8} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 25 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 25 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
                                            &= 25 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$25 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of people she asked liked the Tin Man best ?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{\% people that liked Toto best}       &= \dfrac {2} {8} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 100} {8} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 25 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 25 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {4}} \\\\

\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 100} {8} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 25 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 25 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
                                            &= 25 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$24$ people said that they liked Dorothy best.

How many more people liked Dorothy than the Scarecrow?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of people $=a$

Number of people that liked scarecrow $=\dfrac{a}{8}$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {4} {8} \ \text {of} \ a                 &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {4} {8} \times a                         &= 24 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {24 \times 8} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {24 \times 4 \times 2} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {24 \times \cancel{4} \times 2} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
a                                               &= 24 \times  2 \\\\
a                                               &= 48
\end{aligned}
$

Total number of people $= 48$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{People that liked scarecrow}              &= \dfrac{a}{8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac{48}{8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {6 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {6 \times \cancel{8}} {\cancel{8}} \\\\
                                                &= 6 \\\\
\text {Difference}                              &= 24 - 6 \\\\
                                                &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of people $=a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

a                                               &= \dfrac {24 \times \cancel{4} \times 2} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
a                                               &= 24 \times  2 \\\\

\end{aligned}
$

                                                &= 6 \\\\

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_g2 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>0E054B14-2436-4D6D-9E10-3C0CF4470C70</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2:1:31</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tvolume
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Robert has $70$ identical cube bricks.

He uses some of his bricks to make this cuboid: 

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-31-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-31-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-31-00.png")

From his remaining bricks, he uses some to make the largest cube that he can.

How many bricks does he use to make the cube?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Volume of a cuboid} = \text{length} \times \text{width} \times \text{height}$


ABBR: $\text{Volume of a cube} = (\text{Side}) ^ { 3 }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Bricks used to make cuboid}   &= 4 \times 4 \times 2 \\\\
                                    &= 32 \\\\
\text{Bricks left}                  &= 70 - 32 \\\\
                                    &= 38 \\\\
\text{Maximum length of the cube}   &= \sqrt[3]{38} \\\\
                                    &\approx 3 \\\\
\text{Bricks used for cube}         &= 3 \times 3 \times 3 \\\\
                                    &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_volume_cuboid
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_volume_cube
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Bricks used to make cuboid}   &= 4 \times 4 \times 2 \\\\
                                    &= 32 \\\\
\text{Bricks left}                  &= 70 - 32 \\\\
                                    &= 38 \\\\
\text{Maximum length of the cube}   &= \sqrt[3]{38} \\\\
                                    &\approx 3 \\\\
\text{Bricks used for cube}         &= 3 \times 3 \times 3 \\\\
                                    &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$27$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-32-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-32-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-32-00.png")

Here is a table showing the number of minutes which she 
spent practising her violin on $5 \ \text{days}$ last week.

|day        |practice time (in minutes)|
|:---------:|:------------------------:|
|Monday     |$10$                      |
|Tuesday    |$14$                      |
|Wednesday  |$6$                       |
|Thursday   |$10$                      |
|Friday     |$15$                      |


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out her total practice time for these $5 \ \text{days}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total practice time}     &= 10 + 14 + 6 + 10 + 15 \\\\
                                &= 55 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$55 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total practice time}     &= 10 + 14 + 6 + 10 + 15 \\\\
                                &= 55 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$55 \ \text{min}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out her mean (average) practice time for these $5 \ \text{days}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Using value from the answer above,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Average practice time}   &= \dfrac {55} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {5 \times 11} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 11} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                &= 11 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Average practice time}   &= \dfrac {55} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {5 \times 11} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 11} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                &= 11 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Vanessa also practised on Saturday.

Her mean practice time for all $6 \ \text{days}$ was $15 \ \text{minutes}$.

Work out for how many minutes Vanessa practised on Saturday.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let number of minutes Vanessa practiced on Saturday $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {10 + 14 + 6 + 10 + 15 + a} {6}          &= 15 \\\\
\dfrac {55 + a} {6}                             &= 15 \\\\
55 + a                                          &= 15 \times 6 \\\\
55 + a                                          &= 90 \\\\
a                                               &= 90 - 55 \\\\
a                                               &= 35 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

Practise on Saturday $= 35 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$35 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let number of minutes Vanessa practiced on Saturday $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {10 + 14 + 6 + 10 + 15 + a} {6}          &= 15 \\\\
\dfrac {55 + a} {6}                             &= 15 \\\\
55 + a                                          &= 15 \times 6 \\\\
55 + a                                          &= 90 \\\\
a                                               &= 90 - 55 \\\\
a                                               &= 35 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Practise on Saturday $= 35 \ \text{min}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$35 \ \text{min}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question question'>

In a survey of $40$ people, $23$ were male.

Of the females, $11$ were born in the UK.

Altogether, $28$ people were born in the UK.

Some of this information has been put into the table below.

Complete the table.

|                 |  male  | female   | Total  |
|:---------------:|:------:|:--------:|:------:|
| Born in UK      |        |    $11$  |        |
| Not born in UK  |        |          |        |
| Total           |$23$    |          | $40$   |



</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's complete the table.

|         |    Born in UK                       |   Not born in UK                    |   Total                             |
|:-----:  |:----------------:                   |:---------------:                    |:--------:                           |
| Males   | $28 - 11 = 17 \ \footnotesize{(5)}$ | $23 - 17 = 6 \ \footnotesize{(6)}$  | $23 \ \footnotesize{(2)}$           |
| Females | $11 \ \footnotesize{(3)}$           | $12 - 6 = 6 \ \footnotesize{(8)}$   | $40 - 23 = 17 \ \footnotesize{(9)}$ |
| Total   | $28 \ \footnotesize{(4)}$           | $40 - 28 = 12 \ \footnotesize{(7)}$ | $40 \ \footnotesize{(1)}$           |

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

|         |    Born in UK  |   Not born in UK |   Total  |
|:-----:  |:--------------:|:---------------: |:--------:|
| Males   | $17$           | $6$              | $23$     |
| Females | $11$           | $6$              | $17$     |
| Total   | $28$           | $12$             | $40$     |

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let's complete the table.
<emptyline>
|         |    Born in UK                       |   Not born in UK                    |   Total                             |
|:-----:  |:----------------:                   |:---------------:                    |:--------:                           |
| Males   | $28 - 11 = 17 \ \footnotesize{(5)}$ | $23 - 17 = 6 \ \footnotesize{(6)}$  | $23 \ \footnotesize{(2)}$           |
| Females | $11 \ \footnotesize{(3)}$           | $12 - 6 = 6 \ \footnotesize{(8)}$   | $40 - 23 = 17 \ \footnotesize{(9)}$ |
| Total   | $28 \ \footnotesize{(4)}$           | $40 - 28 = 12 \ \footnotesize{(7)}$ | $40 \ \footnotesize{(1)}$           |
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>|         |    Born in UK  |   Not born in UK |   Total  |
|:-----:  |:--------------:|:---------------: |:--------:|
| Males   | $17$           | $6$              | $23$     |
| Females | $11$           | $6$              | $17$     |
| Total   | $28$           | $12$             | $40$     |
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

On Monday, Helen hangs $8$ shirts and $4$ jumpers on the washing line.

How many pegs does she use?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Pegs used} &= 8 \times 3 + 4 \times 2 \\\\
                 &= 24 + 8 \\\\
                 &= 32
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Pegs used} &= 8 \times 3 + 4 \times 2 \\\\
                 &= 24 + 8 \\\\
                 &= 32
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$32$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

On Wednesday, Helen hangs $5$ shirts and some jumpers on the line.

She uses $21$ pegs altogether.

How many jumpers does Helen hang on the line?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of jumpers hung $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times 5 + 2 \times a &= 21 \\\\
15 + 2 a                &= 21 \\\\
2 a                     &= 21 - 15 \\\\
2 a                     &= 6 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {6} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                       &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

Number of jumpers $=3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of jumpers hung $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times 5 + 2 \times a &= 21 \\\\
15 + 2 a                &= 21 \\\\
2 a                     &= 21 - 15 \\\\
2 a                     &= 6 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {6} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                       &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of jumpers $=3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

On Friday, Helen uses $65$ pegs.

She hangs an equal number of shirts and jumpers on the line.

How many shirts does she hang on the washing line on Friday?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of jumpers  $= a$

Number of shirts  $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times a + 2 \times a &= 65 \\\\
5 a                     &= 65 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {65} {5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {13 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {13 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

Number of Shirts $= 13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13, \ 13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of jumpers  $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

5 a                     &= 65 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {65} {5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {13 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {13 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of Shirts $= 13$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

When it is $16.42$ in London, what time is it in New York?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time in New York} &= 16{:}42 - 5 \ \text{hours}  \\\\
                        &= 11{:}42
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11{:}42$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time in New York} &= 16{:}42 - 5 \ \text{hours}  \\\\
                        &= 11{:}42
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11{:}42$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

When it is $12.37$ in New York, what time is it in Addis Ababa?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time in Addis Ababa} &= 12{:}37 + 5 \ \text{hours}+ 2 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                           &= 12{:}37 + 7 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                           &= 19{:}37
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19{:}37$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time in Addis Ababa} &= 12{:}37 + 5 \ \text{hours}+ 2 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                           &= 12{:}37 + 7 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                           &= 19{:}37
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$19{:}37$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

An aeroplane leaves Addis Ababa at $19.00$ on Tuesday and travels to New York.

The whole journey takes $14 \ \text{hours}$.

At what time and on which day does it arrive in New York?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Arrival Time} &= 19{:}00 + 14 \ \text{hours} - 7 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                    &= 19{:}00 + 7  \ \text{hours}\\\\
                    &= 2{:}00 \\\\
\text {Day of Arrival} &= \text {Wednesday}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Time $=$ $2{:}00$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Day $=$ Wednesday

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Arrival Time} &= 19{:}00 + 14 \ \text{hours} - 7 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                    &= 19{:}00 + 7  \ \text{hours}\\\\
                    &= 2{:}00 \\\\
\text {Day of Arrival} &= \text {Wednesday}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Time</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2{:}00$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Day</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Wednesday
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2:1:39</p>

<div class='rag'>

</li>
<li>
tsimpleword
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A photograph, which measures $12 \ \text{centimetres}$ by $15 \ \text{centimetres}$, is 
mounted on a piece of red card so that there is a border 
of $3 \ \text{centimetres}$ all the way round the photograph.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-39-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-39-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-39-00.png")

What area of red card is showing?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Length of the card}      &= 15 + 2 \times 3 \\\\
                                &= 15 + 6 \\\\
                                &= 21 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Width of the card}       &= 12 + 2 \times 3 \\\\
                                &= 12 + 6 \\\\
                                &= 18 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text{Area of the red card}     &= \text{Total area} - \text{Area of photograph} \\\\
                                &= 21 \times 18 - 15 \times 12 \\\\
                                &= 378 - 180 \\\\
                                &= 198 \ \text{cm} ^ 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$198 \ \text{cm} ^ 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Length of the card}      &= 15 + 2 \times 3 \\\\
                                &= 15 + 6 \\\\
                                &= 21 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Width of the card}       &= 12 + 2 \times 3 \\\\
                                &= 12 + 6 \\\\
                                &= 18 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text{Area of the red card}     &= \text{Total area} - \text{Area of photograph} \\\\
                                &= 21 \times 18 - 15 \times 12 \\\\
                                &= 378 - 180 \\\\
                                &= 198 \ \text{cm} ^ 2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$198 \ \text{cm} ^ 2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Suki writes a list of all the whole numbers from $1$ to $19$ inclusive.

She realises that, since the numbers from $10$ to $19$ have $2$ digits 
each, she has written down $29$ digits in total.

Suki continues her list until she has written down all of the whole 
numbers from $1$ to $99$ inclusive.

</div>

<div class='question question'>

On planet Dichotomy, there are two tribes, Honestians and Liarists.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-41-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-41-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-41-00.png")

Honestians always tell the truth.

Liarists always lie.


The first $2$ people you meet are called Paul and Simon.

Paul says, “We are both liarists.”

Work out which tribe each person belongs to.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working placeholder'>

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>
